\tdim{Ahih hangin} & adversative connector & \tdim{Ahih hangin};
\tdim{Ahih hangin kuamah in dawng lo hi} & supported with caveat &
internally analyzable as \tdim{ahih} + \tdim{hang-in} \\
\tdim{ahih kei leh} & conditional-adversative connector &
\tdim{ahih kei leh}; \tdim{tua ahih kei leh} & supported with caveat &
not a simple coordinator; overlaps conditional and negation material \\
\end{longtable}
}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{NP coordination with
\tdim{le}}{NP coordination with }}\label{np-coordination-with}

The clearest NP coordination anchor is \tdim{vantung le leitung}
\glossquote{heaven and earth}, where \tdim{le} links two noun phrases
without clause-linkage ambiguity.

Genesis 1:1 supplies the OT anchor.

\begin{exe}
\ex \label{ex:coord-le-np}
\gll \tdimword{vàn-tùng} \tdimword{lè} \tdimword{léi-tùng} \\
     sky-on and land-on \\
\glt \glossquote{heaven and earth} (Genesis 1:1)
\end{exe}

Matthew 24:35 gives a Gospel comparandum with the same NP-coordination
profile.

\begin{exe}
\ex \label{ex:coord-le-np-matt24-35}
\gll \tdimword{Vàn} \tdimword{lè} \tdimword{léi} \\
     sky and buy \\
\glt \glossquote{heaven and earth} (Matthew 24:35)
\end{exe}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{Conditional and boundary
\tdim{leh}}{Conditional and boundary }}\label{conditional-and-boundary}

\tdim{Leh} \glossquote{if / when} is kept as boundary material here
rather than as a generalized simple clause coordinator.

Genesis 13:9 keeps a controlled conditional window visible.

\begin{exe}
\ex \label{ex:coord-leh-boundary}
\gll \tdimword{véi-lam} \tdimword{nà} \tdimword{lāk} \tdimword{lèh} \tdimword{kèi} \tdimword{taklam-àh} \tdimword{kà} \tdimword{pái} \tdimword{dìng} \tdimword{hì} \\
     faint-direction \textsc{2sg} among and \textsc{neg} right-\textsc{loc} \textsc{1sg} go \textsc{irr} \textsc{decl} \\
\glt \glossquote{if thou wilt take the left hand, then I will go to the right} (Genesis 13:9)
\end{exe}

No equally clean Gospel example is currently used for this construction.

The same row keeps an export caution visible: \tdim{kei} is glossed as
\tdim{NEG} in this selected span even though the wider context is
first-person future movement.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{Sequential \tdim{a} and agreement false
friends}{Sequential  and agreement false friends}}\label{sequential-and-agreement-false-friends}

Sequential \tdim{a} \glossquote{sequential linker with 3SG/FUNC overlap}
remains caveated boundary evidence only.

Genesis 2:10 keeps one sequential window visible.

\begin{exe}
\ex \label{ex:coord-a-sequential-boundary}
\gll \tdimword{luang} \tdimword{à} \tdimword{túa} \tdimword{mùn} \tdimword{pàn-ìn} \tdimword{gun} \tdimword{hòng} \tdimword{kikhen-ìn} \\
     flow \textsc{3sg} \textsc{dist} place \textsc{abl}-\textsc{erg} river \textsc{3→1} separate-\textsc{cvb} \\
\glt \glossquote{and from thence it was parted} (Genesis 2:10)
\end{exe}

No equally clean Gospel example is currently used for this construction.

The false-friend control \tdim{a piangsak}
\glossquote{3SG/FUNC + create} shows why raw \tdim{a} harvesting is
unsafe in coordinator analysis.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{Deferred \tdim{mawh}
material}{Deferred  material}}\label{deferred-material-11}

\tdim{mawh} \glossquote{disjunction material (currently deferred)}
remains deferred in this section because the controlled row still
exports lexical material (\tdim{sin / V}) rather than a clean
disjunction or alternative-question construction.

Report-only schematic strings such as \tdim{mi mawh ganhing mawh} and
\tdim{pai ding mawh om ding mawh} are not promoted as grammar evidence
here.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{Adversative
\tdim{Ahih hangin}}{Adversative }}\label{adversative}

\tdim{Ahih hangin} \glossquote{but / however} is the current adversative
connector anchor, while still carrying an internal-analysis caution.

Genesis 3:4 supplies the OT anchor.

\begin{exe}
\ex \label{ex:coord-ahih-hangin}
\gll \tdimword{Àhìh} \tdimword{hàng-ìn} \\
     be.\textsc{3sg}.\textsc{rel} because-\textsc{erg} \\
\glt \glossquote{but} (Genesis 3:4)
\end{exe}

Mark 3:4 gives a Gospel comparandum with the same connector.

\begin{exe}
\ex \label{ex:coord-ahih-hangin-mark3-4}
\gll \tdimword{Àhìh} \tdimword{hàng-ìn} \tdimword{kuamah} \tdimword{ìn} \tdimword{dawng} \tdimword{lò} \tdimword{hì.} \\
     be.\textsc{3sg}.\textsc{rel} because-\textsc{erg} nobody \textsc{erg} receive \textsc{neg} \textsc{decl} \\
\glt \glossquote{but no one answered.} (Mark 3:4)
\end{exe}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{Conditional-adversative
\tdim{ahih kei leh}}{Conditional-adversative }}\label{conditional-adversative}

\tdim{ahih kei leh} \glossquote{otherwise / if not} remains
conditional-adversative boundary material and should not be flattened
into simple coordination.

Exodus 12:3 gives the OT boundary anchor.

\begin{exe}
\ex \label{ex:coord-ahih-kei-leh}
\gll \tdimword{àhìh} \tdimword{kèi} \tdimword{lèh} \\
     be.\textsc{3sg}.\textsc{rel} \textsc{neg} and \\
\glt \glossquote{otherwise / if not} (Exodus 12:3)
\end{exe}

Matthew 11:3 gives a Gospel comparandum in the same
conditional-adversative frame.

\begin{exe}
\ex \label{ex:coord-ahih-kei-leh-matt11-3}
\gll \tdimword{túa} \tdimword{àhìh} \tdimword{kèi} \tdimword{lèh} \\
     \textsc{dist} be.\textsc{3sg}.\textsc{rel} \textsc{neg} and \\
\glt \glossquote{or else / if not} (Matthew 11:3)
\end{exe}

\subsubsection{Deferred material}\label{deferred-material-12}

Several issues remain outside the present account. This section does not
reopen \tdim{ciangin} \glossquote{when}, broader \tdim{hangin}
\glossquote{because}, temporal or causal subordination, or full converb
chaining. Sentence-final particles and broader discourse-organization
domains likewise remain outside this account.

\subsection{Reduplication}\label{reduplication}

The current reduplication evidence is strongest for full-reduplication
intensification with \tdim{mahmah} \glossquote{very, truly} and support
from \tdim{taktak} \glossquote{truly, certainly}, while distributive
\tdim{peuhpeuh} \glossquote{each\textasciitilde{}each / every, each},
syntactic \tdim{ni ni} \glossquote{day day / day by day}, and forms such
as \tdim{leuleu} \glossquote{ITER\textasciitilde{}ITER / gradually}
remain secondary, boundary, or deferred material.

\subsubsection{Overview of reduplication in
Tedim}\label{overview-of-reduplication-in-tedim}

This section gives a narrow grammar-facing account of reduplication in
Tedim. The core evidence is strongest for intensifier-like full
reduplication with \tdim{mahmah} \glossquote{very, truly} and support
from \tdim{taktak} \glossquote{truly, certainly}. Secondary distributive
material with \tdim{peuhpeuh}
\glossquote{each\textasciitilde{}each / every, each} is kept visible but
does not lead the analysis.

The section keeps the analysis deliberately small. It distinguishes core
evidence from boundary and deferred material, and it does not treat
reduplication as a complete derivational, TAM/aspect, VP-structure,
expressive-morphology, or dictionary-entry system.

\paragraph{Reduplication inventory}\label{reduplication-inventory}

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}

\endlastfoot
\tdim{mahmah} / \tdim{mah\textasciitilde{}mah} & full-reduplication
intensifier & \tdim{pha mahmah hi} & core anchor & none at the current
evidence level \\
\tdim{taktak} / \tdim{tak\textasciitilde{}tak} & full-reduplication
intensifier support & \tdim{Esau taktak na hi hiam?} & support &
context-sensitive lexical contribution remains \\
\tdim{peuhpeuh} / \tdim{peuh\textasciitilde{}peuh} & distributive or

\subparagraph{Full reduplication as
intensification}\label{full-reduplication-as-intensification}

\tdim{mahmah} is the main full-reduplication intensifier anchor.

Its controlled segmentation is \tdim{mah\textasciitilde{}mah}, with
gloss/function \tdim{EMPH\textasciitilde{}EMPH / very, truly}.

The worked example \tdim{pha mahmah hi} remains central in this section,
with gloss \glossquote{good very DECL}.

\begin{exe}
\ex \label{ex:red-mahmah-gen1-31}
\gll \tdimword{hòih} \tdimword{sá} \tdimword{màhmàh} \tdimword{hì} \\
     good flesh very \textsc{decl} \\
\glt \glossquote{it was very good} (Genesis 1:31)
\end{exe}

Matthew 2:3 gives a Gospel comparandum for the same intensifier pattern.

\begin{exe}
\ex \label{ex:red-mahmah-matt2-3}
\gll \tdimword{lamdang-sá} \tdimword{màhmàh} \\
     different-\textsc{intns} very \\
\glt \glossquote{very alarmed / greatly troubled} (Matthew 2:3)
\end{exe}

\tdim{Taktak} is the closest support row for the same intensifying
pattern.

Its controlled segmentation is \tdim{tak\textasciitilde{}tak}, with
gloss/function \tdim{TRUE\textasciitilde{}TRUE / truly, certainly}.

\begin{exe}
\ex \label{ex:red-taktak-gen27-24}
\gll \tdimword{Esau} \tdimword{tak-tak} \tdimword{nà} \tdimword{hì} \tdimword{hìam?} \\
     \textsc{esau} great\textasciitilde{}REDUP \textsc{2sg} \textsc{decl} \textsc{q} \\
\glt \glossquote{Are you really Esau?} (Genesis 27:24)
\end{exe}

Matthew 27:54 gives a Gospel support row for the same \tdim{taktak}
pattern.

\begin{exe}
\ex \label{ex:red-taktak-matt27-54}
\gll \tdimword{Pasian'} \tdimword{Tá-pà} \tdimword{tak-tak} \tdimword{màh} \tdimword{àhì} \tdimword{hì} \\
     God.\textsc{poss} child-male great\textasciitilde{}REDUP \textsc{emph} be.\textsc{3sg} \textsc{decl} \\
\glt \glossquote{Truly this was the Son of God} (Matthew 27:54)
\end{exe}

These rows support a narrow claim: full reduplication can mark
intensification. They do not by themselves define the full reduplication
system.

\subparagraph{Secondary distributive
reduplication}\label{secondary-distributive-reduplication}

\tdim{Peuhpeuh} remains secondary distributive evidence, with controlled
segmentation \tdim{peuh\textasciitilde{}peuh} and gloss/function
\tdim{EACH\textasciitilde{}EACH / every, each}.

\begin{exe}
\ex \label{ex:red-peuhpeuh-gen18-14}
\gll \tdimword{Nà} \tdimword{khàt} \tdimword{peuhpeuh} \tdimword{Topa} \tdimword{à} \tdimword{dìng-ìn} \tdimword{hàk-sá} \tdimword{lúa} \tdimword{àhì} \tdimword{hìam?} \\
     \textsc{2sg} one every Lord \textsc{3sg} \textsc{irr}-\textsc{erg} difficult-\textsc{past} exceed be.\textsc{3sg} \textsc{q} \\
\glt \glossquote{Is anything too hard for the Lord?} (Genesis 18:14)
\end{exe}

Matthew 10:11 supplies a Gospel comparandum for secondary distributive
use.

\begin{exe}
\ex \label{ex:red-peuhpeuh-matt10-11}
\gll \tdimword{khùa} \tdimword{khàt} \tdimword{peuhpeuh} \tdimword{nà} \tdimword{tun} \tdimword{ùh} \tdimword{cìang-ìn} \\
     town one every \textsc{2sg} arrive 2/3PL then-\textsc{erg} \\
\glt \glossquote{whichever town you enter} (Matthew 10:11)
\end{exe}

This construction remains secondary because it stays close to quantifier
or free-choice behavior. It is relevant to reduplication but not
promoted as the main anchor.

\subparagraph{Boundary and deferred
material}\label{boundary-and-deferred-material}

\tdim{Ni ni} \glossquote{day day / day by day} remains syntactic or
adverbial boundary material. No equally clean Gospel example is
currently used for this construction.

\tdim{Leuleu} \glossquote{ITER\textasciitilde{}ITER / gradually} remains
TAM/aspect or VP-boundary material; Acts 28:13 (\tdim{pai leuleu-in}) is
retained as prose-only boundary evidence rather than as a core formal
example.

\tdim{Gengen}
\glossquote{speak\textasciitilde{}speak / saying repeatedly},
\tdim{kawikawi} \glossquote{side\textasciitilde{}side / all around},
\tdim{theithei}
\glossquote{KNOW\textasciitilde{}KNOW / adverbial-modal boundary},
\tdim{bangbang} \glossquote{kind\textasciitilde{}kind / various},
\tdim{bekbek} \glossquote{only\textasciitilde{}only}, \tdim{zenzen}
\glossquote{completely}, and \tdim{tuamtuam} \glossquote{various kinds}
remain boundary-controlled or deferred. These rows are
lexicalized-looking or report-only in the current evidence and are not
promoted as core grammar evidence here.

Several issues remain outside the present account. This section does not
present a full reduplication, derivation, TAM/aspect, VP-structure,
expressive-morphology, or dictionary-entry system.

draft.
